ṉār.  aru. aru. cōmacuntarañ ceṭṭiyār uḷḷiṭṭa cilar cūḻntaṉar.  eṅkaḷ uṟiviṭam ampalam āyiṟṟu;  ciṟṟampala
māyiṟṟu;  ōviyak kūṭalāyiṟṟu;  kāviya niliyamāyiṟṟu.  eṅkaḷ eṇṇamum pēccum ōviyattil nuḻintaṉa;  tillik kūttaṉiṭañ ceṉṟaṉa.  yāṉ tilli pōntēṉ;  kūttaṉ ōviyattik kaṇṭēṉ;  kāṭci amiti yaḷittatu.  iṉpūṭ
ṭiyatu eṉṟu cokkaliṅkaṉār coṟṟār.  pēcukiṟavar cokkaliṅkamā eṉṟu nōkkiṉēṉ.  āmām!  pēcukiṟavar cokkaliṅkamē.  cokkaliṅkaṉār kalvi kēḷviyuṭiyavar;  ārāycciyāḷar;  kāytal uvattaliṉṟi etiyuñ cintittu muṭivu kāṇpavar;  etaṟkum virivil nekiḻntu kaṇṇīr viṭātavar.  attakiya oruvar tiruvācakap pāṭṭil ami
kiṟār;  tillikkūttaṉ ōviyattil mūḻkukiṟār.  itu tāṉē civam.  ivvāṟē ceṭṭiyāriṭaṅ kūṟiṉēṉ.  avar ippoḻutu tamiḻ vakuppu naṭāttukiṟār eṉṟu kēḷvi
yuṟṟēṉ.
cokkaliṅkaṉār kāṅkira toṇṭāṟṟic ciṟi pukuntavar;  kārikkuṭi (nakarac) capit talivarāyiruntavar;  ippoḻutu pāṭṭilum ōviyattilum mūḻkukiṟār;  kaliyil mūḻkukiṟār;  tamiḻ vakuppu naṭattukiṟār.  avar vāyilākac ceṭṭināṭu tamiḻk kaliyil eḻucci peṟumeṉṉum uṟuti eṉakku ēṟpaṭṭatu.

eṅkaḷ uriyāṭaliṉpōtu aru. aru. cōmacuntarañ ceṭṭiyār taṇṇār tamiḻaḷikkun taṇpāṇṭi nāṭ

ṭēṉ.  avar tamiḻ aḷikkum nāṭṭāṉ eṉṟār.  aṅkē tamiḻt tāyiṉ oḷi kāṉṟatu.  cōmacuntarañ ceṭṭiyār pakkam tirumpi, ummi yāṉ maṟantēṉilli.  umatu piḷḷimik kōlam niṉiviluṟukiṟatu.  iṭiyil maṉam eṅkeṅkēyō cuḻaṉṟatu eṉṟu kēḷviyuṟṟēṉ.  nēṟṟu nīṅkaḷ viḻāvilē.

		toṭiyiṉ payaṉē naṟipaḻutta

		oruvaṉ tiruvuḷ ḷattilaḻa
	koḻuka eḻutip pārttirukkum
		uyirō viyamē matukaramvāy

		vañcik koṭiyē varukavē

eṉṉum pāṭṭip pāṭiyapōtu ummip paḻiya cōma cuntaramākavē kaṇṭēṉ.  ippoḻutu uriyāṭalil umatu niliyit terintu koṇṭēṉ;  ummit tavaṟāka uṇarn
tēṉ;  tamiḻi vaḷaruṅkaḷ;  tamiḻkkaliyi vaḷaruṅkaḷ;  teṉpāṇṭi nāṭṭāṉ kaliyi vaḷaruṅkaḷ.  nīṅkaḷ kaliñar.  kaliñarākavē kaṭaṉāṟṟuṅkaḷ eṉṟu moḻin
tēṉ.
āṉantam
eṉ āṉantam muṭivuriyil veḷivantatu.  iṉṟikku irupattintāṇṭukku muṉṉar nām vititta viti vaḷarntē varukiṟatu.  ceṭṭināṭu kalināṭākum eṉṉum makiḻcci
yuṭaṉ tirucirāppaḷḷi cērntēṉ.
tiricci
tiricciyil aṉparum vīrarumākiya vicuvanātaṉ illattil taṅkiṉēṉ.  vīrar muyaṟciyāl ciṭṭi ameccuvar caṅkattiṉ cārpil (2.1.1944) īṇṭiya peruṅkūṭṭatil nakara
capi āṇiyar pi.si. talivar talimiyil tamiḻppaṟṟu eṉṟa poruḷpaṟṟip pēciṉēṉ.  aṅkiruntu aṭutta nāḷ tiruttavattuṟi eṉṉum lālkuṭikkup pōṉēṉ.  tiru
vaḷḷuvar kaḻakattil talimi pūṇṭu, māṇākkar aḷitta vāḻtti ēṟṟuc ceṉṉi aṇintēṉ.
maṇiviḻāvukkup piṉṉart tamiḻ nāṭṭil yāṉ eṉṉa kaṇṭēṉ?  moḻiyuṇarcci kaṇṭēṉ;  cāti mata uṇarvu, orumip pāṭṭukkuk kēṭu cūḻtaliyuṅ kaṇṭēṉ;  orumip
pāṭṭik kaliyāl uṇṭupaṇṇutal kūṭumeṉṟu uṇarkiṟēṉ.
antaṇmi
eṉ vāḻkkik kuṟippukkaḷ cilavaṟṟip potu makkaḷ muṉṉē kiṭattiyuḷḷēṉ;  iṉṉuñ cila kuṟippukkaḷ eṉ karuviluḷḷaṉa.  kākita nerukkut tīrnta piṉṉi avikaḷum veḷivaralām.
palatiṟat tollikaḷiṭi eṉ vāḻkki vaḷarntatu;  ataṉāl atu poruṭ perukkil puraḷātatāyiṟṟu;  toṇ
ṭiṉ perukkil puraḷvatāyiṟṟu.  vāḻkkiyiṉ aṭivu poruṭperukkāyiṉ eṉṉuṭiyatu tōlvi eytiyatākum;  toṇṭāyiṉ atu veṟṟi eytiyatākum.
toṇṭiṉ cēy etu?  antaṇmi.  toṇṭu, antaṇmi ūṟṟit tiṟantatu.  antaṇmi aruviyāy ōṭukiṟatu.  atu veḷḷamātal vēṇṭum.  eṉatu vāḻkki orōvaḻi antaṇac celvattip peṟṟatu.  avvantaṇac celvam muḻunili eytum muyaṟciyil eṉatu vāḻkkiyiṉ eñciya pakutiyum īṭupaṭumāṟu āṇṭavaṉ aruḷ kāppāṉāka.
ippiṟaviyil eṉatu vāḻkki antaṇac celvam peṟutaṟkut tuṇi niṉṟa kalvi kēḷvikaṭkum, nalliṇak
kattukkum, ilvāḻkkikkum, iyakkaṅkaṭkum, piṟavaṟṟiṟkum eṉatu vāḻttu uriyatāka.
paramaṇṭalaṅkaḷilirukkiṟa eṅkaḷ pitāvē, ummuṭiya nāmam paricuttappaṭuvatāka.
ummuṭiya rājyam varuvatāka, ummuṭiya cittam paramaṇṭalattilē ceyyappaṭukiṟatupōlap pūmiyilēyum ceyyappaṭuvatāka.
aṉṟaṉṟu vēṇṭiya eṅkaḷ ākāratti iṉṟum eṅkaḷukkut tārum.
eṅkaḷ kaṭaṉāḷikaḷukku nāṅkaḷ maṉṉikkiṟatu pōla eṅkaḷ kaṭaṉkaḷi eṅkaḷukku maṉṉiyum.

12-9-1881 tamiyaṉār tiru.vi. ulakanāta mutaliyār piṟappu.  26-8-1883 tiru.vi.ka piṟappu.  1890 tuḷḷam kirāmattiliruntu irāyappēṭṭikkuk kuṭiyiruppi māṟṟutal.  1891 irāyap pēṭṭi āriyaṉ pirimeri pāṭacāliyil cērtal.  1894 veli kalācāliyil cērtal.  cila mātaṅkaṭkuḷ pattiya muṟivāl kāl kikaḷ muṭaṅkutal.  1894-1898 paṭippukku ōyvu.  1898 mīṇṭum veli kalācāliyil cērtal.  ṭicampar 1901 katirivēṟ piḷḷiyavarkaḷi mutaṉ mutal cantittal.  1901-1906 katirivēlariṉ māṇākkarāyp payiṟci peṟutal.  1903 srī pālacuppiramaṇiya paktajaṉa capi toṭaṅkutal.  1904 katirivēṟ piḷḷi vaḻakkil cāṉṟu kūṟutal, meṟṟi kulēṣaṉ parīkṣikkuc cellat tērnteṭukkappaṭāmi, paḷḷikkūṭap paṭippiṉ muṭivu.  11-9-1905 tantiyār civapata maṭital.  1906 pukkīppiṅ payiṟci peṟṟut tēṟutal.  26-3-1907 katirivēṟ piḷḷi kantaṉaṭi cērtal.  1907 kaṭaṟkariyil vipiṉa cantirapālar coṟpoḻivukaḷik kēṭṭal.  1907-1908 peṉsar kampeṉiyil vēlipārttal.  1908 katirivēṟ piḷḷi caritam iyaṟṟal.  1908-1910 mayili makā vitvāṉ taṇikācala mutaliyāriṭam cittānta cāttiram payilutal.  1908-1910 umāpati kuruppirakācam pira toṭaṅkip periya purāṇa uri veḷiyiṭal.  1908-1915 tiruvallikkēṇic civaṉaṭiyār tirukkūṭṭac cārpil periyapurāṇam mutaṟ kāṇṭam piracaṅkam ceytu muṭittal, 1908 jaṭi sar ṭi. catāciva jayaravarkaḷ naṭpup peṟutal.  1910 aṉṉipesaṇṭ ammiyāric cantittal.  1910-1916 āyiram viḷakkup paḷḷik kūṭattil āciriyarāyp paṇipurital.  ṭicampar 1911 civa cittānta makā camājattil mutaṉ mutal coṟpoḻi vāṟṟutal.  13-9-1912 kamalāmpikiyammiyārit tirumaṇam purital.  1912-1918 illaṟam naṭattutal.  ṭicampar 1912 kāñci purattil maṇam pūṇṭi kumāracāmi piḷḷiyiṉ naṭpuk peṟutal.  1913 paṭṭiṉattār pāṭalkaṭku uri eḻutal.  ṭicampar 1913 srīlasrī ñāṉiyār cuvāmikaḷ talimiyil civacittānta makācamāja viḻāvil pēcutal.  1914 cupparāyakāmat cantippu.  1914 e. srīnivāsa aiyaṅkāravarkaḷ naṭpu.  1915 putuppākkam civacuppiramaṇiya paktajaṉa capiyiṉ tōṟṟam. jūṉ 1916 veli kallūriyil talimit tamiḻāciriyarātal.  ṭicampar 1916 jaṭi kaṭciyiṉ piṟappu.  1917 kāñcipurattil vāṭiyāviṉ kēṇmi.  ceṭmpar 1917 araciyaṟṟuṟiyil kaṉṉip pēccu.  cila nāṭkaḷukkuḷ ceṉṉi mākāṇac caṅkattiṉ piṟappu.  navampar 1917 mēṟpaṭi caṅkak kāriyataricikaḷuḷ oruvarātal.  ṭākṭar varatarājalu nāyuṭuvik kāṇṭal.  05-12-1917 veli kallūrit tamiḻāciriyar pataviyiliruntu vilakutal.  7-12-1917 tēcapaktaṉ pattirikāciriya rātal.  8-12-1917 cakkaravartti irāja kōpālācāriyāravar kaḷi mutaṉ mutal kāṇṭal.  27-4-1918 ceṉṉit toḻi lāḷar caṅkam tōṉṟutal.  18-9-1918 maṉiviyār kamalāmpiki yammiyār civapatamaṭital.  28-2-1919 tēcapaktaṉ īṭukāṇam paṟimutal peyyappaṭutal.  18-3-1919 makātmā kāntiyi mutaṉ mutal pārttal.  6-4-1919 cattiyākkiraka nōṉpu nāḷ.  12-6-1919 lārṭ villiṅṭaṉ tēciyap pattirikāciriyarkaṭku eccarikki vaḻaṅkutal.  1919 ī.vē. irāmacāmi nāyakkar naṭpup peṟutal.  17-12-1919 pālakaṅkātara tilakar kāṭci.  11-1-1920 aṉṉi pesaṇṭ ammiyārukku varavēṟpu.  4-7-1920 mattiyat toḻi lāḷar caṅkam piṟattal.  22-7-1920 tēcapaktaṉ āciriyar patavi nīttal.  21-10-1920 pakkiṅkāmmil katavaṭippu.  22-10-1920 navacakti pattiriki toṭaṅkutal.  11-11-1920 toḻilāḷar iyakkat talivarmītu vaḻakku.  1920-1929 pala araciyal makānāṭukaḷil talimi vakittalum paṅku peṟutalum.  27-1-1921 mil katavaṭippiṉ muṭivu.  20-6-1921 mīṇṭum vēli niṟuttam.  5-7-1921 larṭ villiṅṭaṉuṭaṉ cantippu.  9-8-1921 vaḻakku vāpī vāṅkappaṭutal.  10-8-1921 eyliṅ kamiṭṭi vicāraṇi.  29-8-1921 tuppākkip pirayōkam.  ṭicampar 1921 vēli niṟutta muṭivu.  21, 22-11-1925 kāñcipurattil tamiḻ nāṭṭuk kāṅkira makānāṭṭut talimi, vakuppuvārip piratinitittuvam paṟṟi tīrmāṉam nirākarikkappaṭutal, ī.ve.rā. mutaliyōr veḷiyēṟṟam.  1926 teṉ intiya jīvarakṣā capiyiṉ piṟappu.  25-2-1927 cāpūrji caklatvālā cantippu.  9-10-1928 tamiyaṉār putalvaṉ tiru. u. pālacuppira maṇiyaṉ civapatamaṭital.  1931 ceṉṉit toḻilāḷar caṅka niliyat tiṟappu viḻā.  1933 māmaṇṭūril uḻavar vēli niṟuttam.  ṭicampar 1934 civa cittānta makā camājat talimi.  jūli 1937 kāṅkira mantiri capi amittal.  1939 kāṅkira āṭciyil mil vēli niṟuttam.  1940 tiruvoṟṟi yūril pali niṟuttam.  jaṉavari 1941 srīmati irātāmaṇi yammāyārukku navacakti pattirikiyit tarutal.  14-4-1943 ceṉṉit toḻilāḷar caṅka veḷḷi viḻā.  24-8-1943 
tiru. vi. ka. aṟupatu niṟital.  25-8-1943 mutal palanāṭkaḷ maṇi viḻākkūṭṭaṅkaḷ.  27, 28, 29-12-1943 maturiyil civar makānāṭṭut talimi.  30, 31-12-1943 kārikkuṭi hintu matāpimāṉa caṅkattiṉ veḷḷi viḻāt talimi.

1	toḻilāḷar caṅkaṅkaḷ āṅkāṅkē nāṭōṟun tōṉṟivarukiṉṟaṉa.  avikaḷi vaḻituṟiyillāmal vaḷarttu varuvatāl, pala iṭarkaḷ iṭiyil nērntuviṭum.  ātalāl ellāt toḻiṟcaṅkaṅkaṭkum naṭunāyakamāka oru mattiyac caṅkamiruttal vēṇṭum.  accaṅkattil ellāt toḻiṟcaṅkaṅkaḷuñ cērtal vēṇṭum.  mattiyac caṅkam oṉṟiruppatu toḻilāḷar iyakkattukku ākkantēṭuvatākum.  oru mattiyac caṅkam amikka srīmati mirunāḷiṉi caṭṭōpāttiyāyā ceytuvarum muyaṟcikku immakānāṭu tuṇiceyyumeṉṟu nampukiṟēṉ.
1	To
	(KALYANASUNDARA MUDALIAR, Esq, Madras.)
	My Dear Friend,
	     This is your book as much as mine.  If the speeches delivered here achieved any good among out friends at perambur it is due to your excellent translations of them.  What could I have done in the Labour work in this city without you?  you translated my speeches - not my words merely but their very spirit.  We have rejoiced and suffered together with thousands.  Your unobtrusive manner, your loyal support, your sagacious advice have been priceless.  Let me request you to accept this small gift of dedication for your all too splendid services.
	Ever Yours.
	B.P. Wadia.
	     (‘Labour in Madras’ by B.P. Wadia with a foreword by Col. Josiah. 
C. Wedgwood, M.P. (1921.) S. Ganesan & Co., Publishers, Triplicane.S.E.)
1	All these parties were present on the 10th August except the Madras Labour Union President who addressed the Committee the following letter:-
	‘Sir,
	I have the honour to acknowledge the receipt of your letter dated the 6th inst, relating to the sittings of the committee of enquity into the Madras Disturbances of June and July 1921 and to thank you for inviting me to be present at the sid enquiry.
	In reply I am desired by the Madras Labour Union to inform you that the Madras Labour Union as such does not propose to participate in the enquiry, which as the Government order points out, will not extend to the merits and to any disputes between the management of the Buckingham and Carnatic mills and their employees.  The enquiry into the disturbances which took place in the perambur division on and after the 29th June, is a matter of concern to the general public and not to the members of the union as such, and it affects the peace and order of the northern parts of the city.  The Labour Union repudiates any manner of connection with such disturbances as have taken place.  They have since been in part at least a matter of adjudication before the magisterial courts in Madras and evidence has been adduced before these courts at the instance of the police as well as the parties aggrieved and judgements have been pronounced by the Chief Presidency Magistrate of the city in two cases which show clearly the origin of the disturbance and the persons involved in provoking them as well as the conduct of the police in dealing with them.  There are still a number of police proceedings pending before stipendiary and honorary magistrates in respect of this matter in which members of the union are being needlessly harassed and put to great hardship.  In the circumstances, the union is unable to furnish the committee with a list of persons who might be examined as witnessess by the committee and I regret very much to find myself unable to supply you now or later with a list of persons whom I could in the existing state of things suggest for examination as witnessess.
	Yours truly,
	T.V. KALYANASUNDARAM
President, Madras Labour Union.
		   In consequence of this refusal on the part of the Labour Union to participate in the enquiry which was much regretted by the committee announced at the first meeting that they desired it to be known that any accredited representatives of the strikers would by afforded the same facilities in regard to this enquiry, as would have been given to the Madras Labour Union.  The representatives of the Press were asked to give every possible publicity to this announcement.  But unfortunately no representative of the strikers or any section of them appeared during the inquiry...”
	Hindu (14.10.1921)

WELCOME ADDRESS
TO
Sriman T.V. KALYANASUNDARA MUDALIAR,
Madras
1.	Dear Sir, 
		     We, the Chirman and the members of the Municipal Council, Vellore, have great pleasure in extending to you a heartly welcome in our midst this morning.
	     We have been closely watching with keen interest and sympathy your varied public activites since several years past and our firm conviction continues to be that you are a patriot of the first order and that to whatever channels you devote your energies and enthusiasm, you have in mind the national regeneration of our Motherland as your goal.
	     We remember with gratitude the eminent services you have done in the past with great personal sacrifice in strengthening the national movement of the Country and your then eloquent and lucid public utterances in our mother - tongue still ring in our ears as masterful contribution to the Tamil literature to be read and re-read and emulated by every well-wisher of the Country.
	     Your devotion to the cuse of the poor and toiling millions is unrivalled.  You have long been connected with the Trade Union movement in the Presidency and it is, we believe, no exaggeration to say that the status and 
	the position which this body has now gined is due largely to the sustined valuable services rendered by you with unabated enthusiasm and love towards that cuse.  The mantle of the Presidentship of the District Labour Conference which begins its sessions to - day has therefore rightly fallen on 
	your shoulders, and we hope that under your guidance the Conference will have an eventful success this year.
	     In these days of degeneration of our mother-tongue we rejoice to find in you a Tamil scholar of the first rank with rare powers of exposition of our classical literature.  With your facile pen and gift of expression, you have been doing invaluable services to the Country in bringing home to the youths the beauties of our ancient language.
	May the Almighty grant you many more years of healthy and prosperous life.
	We remin, Dear Sir,
	Yours sincerely.
	Vellore	Chirman& Members of the Municipal Council
	10.9.1933 }	Vellore.
2.	Messrs. T.V. KALYANASUNDARA MUDALIAR, Avl.,
		Journalist, Madras
AND
V.V.GIRI Avargal, Barrister at law, Madras.
	Dear Sirs.
	     We deem it a great honour to welcome you to this District and present you with an address on behalf of the District Board.  Both of you are so intimately connected with the development of the Trade Union Movement in this Presidency, that it is in the fitness of things that the first of you have been chosen for the honour of presiding over and that the second of you for opening the labour conference today.
	     You, Mr. Kalyanasundara Mudaliar, have had the unique privilege of being one of the pioneers of the labour movement of this country and one who have been prominently connected with all national movement of this presidency.  Your transparent patriotism and selfless devotion to the cuse of the poor have won for you a name, which conduces ideas of piety, sacrifice, devotion and service in the minds of the several millions of the Tamil land.  Your learning and research have won for you a permanent place in the rich of Tamil Literature.
	     In spite of your poor health, you have never missed an opportunity to popularise and spread the cuse of Tamil and of the truths contined in its
	sacred books.  As the Editor of Desa Bakthan and Navasakthi you have indicated the cuse of nationalism with unfringing courage and devotion.  It is a matter of Peculiar gratification that we  can  count  you  as  one  of  our
	District men in view of the very many intimate connections of interest and men that you have in this district. 
	     May Almighty give you long life and good health to continue the cuse which you have espoused on behalf of the poor and the mother land. ***
vellore	the president & members of the
10th September 1933 }	district BOARD, North Arcot.

	     aṉpumikka aṇṇalē!  taṅkaḷ varuki nalvaravākuka.  in nākiyampatiyil toḻilāḷarkaḷ oṟṟumiyiṉṟi, vaṟumi vāyppaṭṭu muṉṉēṟum vaḻiyaṟiyātu varunti vāḷā irunta kāli, ematu nilikaṇṭiraṅki ēḻikaḷ pāl aṉpukūrntu toḻilāḷariṉ muṉṉēṟṟamē tēcattiṉ muṉṉēṟṟameṉu muṇmiyi ulakaṟiyac ceytu, emmiṭit tōṉṟit tāṅkaḷum, ṭākṭar ña. varatarājarum nikaḻttiya nallurikaḷiṉ palaṉāyk kāṇappeṟṟa iccaṅkattiṉ 7 ām āṇṭu niṟivu viḻāviṉ talimiyit tāṅkaḷ vakikkum pēṟṟip peṟṟami kuṟittu yāṅkaḷ aḷavilā āṉantam aṭikiṉṟōm.
	     tāyiṉuñ ciṟanta tayavuṭip peruma!  nantamiḻ nāṭṭut toḻilāḷar muṉṉēṟṟattil taṅkaṭkuḷḷa ārvattiyum, tāṅkaḷ āṟṟiya arumpaṇiyiyum ēḻiyākiya yāṅkaḷ eṉṟeṉṟum maṟavātiruppataṉṟic ceyyakkūṭiya kimmāṟētumilatu.

1	potumi vēṭṭal
1	I have much pleasure in giving this testimonial to Mr. T.V. Kalyanasundara Mudaliar with regard to his scholarship in Tamil.  On three occasion.  I prdesided at the lectures given by him on Tamil literature and I was struck by the extensive knowledge of that language which he desplayed on those occasions.  He has in him combined the extensive knowledge of a pandit and 
	the systematized scholarship of a modern teacher.  He will I am sure be a valuable acquisition to any college that may require this services as a Teacher of Tamil.
	Thottakkadu House. Madras	T. RAMAKRISHNA PILLAI.
	29.3.1916.
